ParlaMint-HR_2004-07-14-0.u17723	Your Honor, Mr. President, the Honorable Prime Minister, the Honorable MPs and MPs. Here is the proposal for a new Transport Safety Act. We remember that the Transport Safety Act was passed in 1992 and significant amendments to the road safety law were made in 1996. Since the start of the application of certain measures prescribed by this law, such as the introduction of penalty points, stricter penalties for the harsh violation of traffic rules with the possibility of withdrawing a driving licence at the scene of the infringement, the verification of the knowledge of traffic rules, and others, there has been a significant improvement in road safety. Thus, from 975 people killed in car accidents in 1992, the number was reduced to 627 people killed in 2002. Despite the increase in the number of traffic accidents, there was a reduction in the number of people killed and seriously injured. However, there was a deterioration in road safety in 2003, which shows an increase in the number of people killed and the number of injured. In that year, 701 people died, which is more for 74 people than in 2002. Unfortunately, the unfavourable trend of road traffic victims continues in this year. In the first six months, there were 43,000 160 car accidents, 1873 accidents or 4.5% higher compared to the same period in 2003, and 317 people died, or 39 dead or 15% higher, compared to the same period in 2003. And 11 thousand 996 people were injured. This has already prompted that during the previous assembly session, this law was twice on reading changes and additions. And so we had to approach the new law now. If we briefly analyze the most common causes of traffic accidents, then we see that this is an irregular rate, with a share of about 33%. No respect for the advantage of a 13% pass. Driving at an unauthorized distance with a stake of about 10%. Irregular overrun with a 7% stake. The share of alcoholism in car accidents is about 7%. And the share of young drivers in car accidents with dead persons with about 25%. The main characteristics of the road traffic risk situation in the Republic of Croatia are the basic characteristics. A marked deterioration in the security situation is linked to the application of the Law on Infringements of 1 October 2002, the application of which has been put into effect by a series of until then effective measures of the applicable Road Safety Act. Like protective measures, negative points, temporary withdrawal of the driver's license up to 90 days, and another. The current state of implementation of the Road Safety Act is so that, in addition to traffic management and enforcement, all other measures we take remain truly ineligible, given the state of the Criminal Law. The very unfavourable state of traffic on roads with a tendency to apply the non-implementable law was sufficient reasons for the hasty adoption of the new Law, which entirely and which is existentially governed by this area. With the draft road safety law proposal, we have also launched a parallel initiative for amendments and additions to the Law on Infringements. The implementation of the text of the law was attended by several experts from all resolutions who have in any part the points of contact with the Road Safety Act. Translations from Italian, Austrian, Slovenian and other mentioned laws were also used. The text is in line with European Union legislation. The text of the draft bill is a nomotechnical way for the criminal provision to be found immediately from, with material provisions such as nomotechnical arrangements have been approached for practical reasons, since this law uses a wide range of persons, so the law will be able to benefit more easily. In the proposed proposal of the Road Safety Act, the following paper is the most important in relation to the provisions of the current law. The material provisions in the current text of the law have been corrected and supplemented in such a way that they are more understandable and clear with a view to efficiency for transport safety. Local and regional self-government units shall be empowered to organise, in accordance with the regulations, the performance of certain traffic control and management tasks. Then, in order to protect the environment, the obligations of drivers and other road users on the ban on leaving or throwing objects or waste on road and road land are more precisely prescribed, as well as damaged and worn-out vehicles on the road and other surfaces. The age limit of validity of the driving licence is increased from 65 to 75 years of age with its replacement every 10 years. The supervision of the work of car schools and the conduct of driving tests shall be transferred to the Ministry of the Interior, and driving tests in the future will be conducted at test centres designated by the Ministry of Interior, where driver's license will be issued. The obligation to wear a safety helmet for bike drivers is introduced, for walking at night it is prescribed to wear reflective markings on roads which are unlit. It shall be more precise when and in what cases it may be excluded from the traffic of the driver and the vehicle, and the mode and procedure of exclusion. The newspaper introduces the exclusion of riders and passengers of mopeds and motorcycles if they do not wear a helmet. A ban and a penalty for using a cell phone is being imposed. The installation and use of devices which may interfere with the traffic control device shall be prohibited. And what caused the most controversy in the press is the introduction of a complete ban on the proportion of alcohol in the body when operated by a vehicle, and the penalty is graduated from only fines to the maximum penalty provided for in this law. At this point, I must say that not only as Minister of the Interior, but as a doctor, I am for it, because in countless cases as a surgeon I was present when patients were brought in in an alcoholic state that caused car accidents and made me unhappy except myself and many who were not under the influence of alcohol. So, under the influence of alcohol, they have not only made themselves unhappy, but also many innocent victims. In the bill of law, the blades of criminal policy are aimed at the most severe violations, and more severe penalties are foreseen for the so-called so-called. intentional undisciplined drivers, for example parking in a place reserved for disabled persons, mobile use during driving, not tying to a safety belt and other. The fine is proposed in the amount of 500 kuna for not wearing a protective helmet up to 700 kuna. The most severe punishments are intended for the most severe violations. A fine of at least 3 thousand kuna, a ban on driving a motor vehicle for a period of 6 months and 5 negative points are proposed for: Driving under the influence of alcohol when it has more than 1.5 prominence or is under the influence of intoxicating drugs. The drive, the speed in the settlement, which is double the allowable if it crosses a speed greater than 100 km per hour. Running away from the scene of a serious car accident. Refusal of subjection, examination or medical examination, or taking blood and urine to determine presence in the organism of alcohol or intoxicating drugs. For motor vehicle drivers, trams or driving instructors who, for a period of 24 months, are being fined two or more times in law for these offences, it is predicted that the Criminal Court, instead of fines, imposes 60 days in prison. All parallel but also for lower speeds, for less alcohol the penalties mean from 2 thousand kuna or three months three negative points or a thousand kunas 2 to 6 months and two negative points. For traffic offences caused by a traffic accident, stricter penalties and two additional negative points are foreseen. As you can see from the data on traffic accidents and the proposed criminal policies, it is obvious that criminal policy is focused on the main causes of traffic accidents and casualties. With the system of negative points, we especially want to act on drivers who do not change their behavior. Therefore, when we propose a new measure in line with amendments to the Law on infringements of the suspension of the validity of the driver's licence as a final measure with nine negative points. Prior to that, with six negative criminal points, the measures provided for in the consequences of the car accident and the safe driving school would apply. Dear MPs and MPs, I hope that we all understand the gravity of the situation on our roads and that after discussing this law, we will accept this law for the benefit of all our citizens. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2004-07-14-0.u17725	Dear Mr. President of the Parliament, Prime Minister, colleagues and colleagues! The proposed amendments and additions to the Law on Infringements relate to ensuring the effective implementation of the infringement provisions contained in the new proposal of the Road Safety Act. As the Road Safety Act provides for the tightening of criminal sanctions, it was necessary to adopt a provision in the Law on Infringements so as to increase the total maximum fines for natural persons from 10 thousand kuna to 15 thousand kuna, while for legal persons a maximum of 100 thousand to 500 thousand kuna was increased. Provisions concerning the protection of motor vehicle management measures have been amended so that this protection measure can now be prescribed and imposed as a obligatory measure and a new safeguard measure has been prescribed to suspend the validity of the driving licence and this is due to the cumulation of a number of negative points which may be imposed on the perpetrator of the offence and as provided for by the Road Safety Act. In order to ensure the effective application of road safety measures by police for the most serious infringements prescribed by the Road Safety Act, the withdrawal of the driver’s licence was also necessary to amend Article 138 of the Law on Infringements in a way that would ensure the withdrawal of the driver’s licence in duration and in the manner prescribed by the Road Safety Act. And let me tell you something else in the end. This novel, very short novels as you had the opportunity to see in the Law on Infringements, is merely an agreement with the proposed new Law on Road Safety and yet it is important to say that the Ministry of Justice, on behalf of the motions of the Government of the Republic of Croatia, has begun working on a comprehensive amendment and complementing the material and procedural provisions of the Law on Infringements and all in order to achieve greater economicity, urgency and efficiency of criminal sanctioning. In the fall, we can expect a comprehensive new Law on Infractions. Thank you very much.

In particular, the trend of increasingly unfavourable road traffic in 2003 continued in 2004. As a reason for improving the safety of road traffic, it also lists a change in the Law on Infringements applicable since October 2002. ParlaMint-HR_2004-07-14-0.u17729	Your Honor, your Honor, Minister! At the 8th session held on 12 July 2004, the Justice Committee of the Croatian Parliament discussed the draft law amending and supplementing the Law on infringements with the final draft law, which was submitted by the Government of the Republic of Croatia to the President of the Croatian Parliament by its act of 8 July 2004. The Committee discussed the law in question on the basis of its competence as set out in Article 73. Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament as a parent working body. The Proposer's introductory representative pointed out that the adoption of the Road Safety Act should amend certain provisions of the Law on Infringements. The level of fines prescribed above has been increased and natural persons and the range of fines for legal persons and the safeguards for the prohibition of the management of motor vehicles as obligator are also prescribed. It was assessed in the debate that the clumsy formulation was contained in Article 5 and that the amendment should be submitted. It is also necessary to nomotechnically edit Article 32, and in paragraph 3 behind the word "officer" should be added: "is". Following the discussion, the Committee on Justice unanimously decided to propose to the Croatian Assembly the adoption of the Law amending and supplementing the Law on infringements with the next amendment. Amendment 1 to Article 5 In Article 138, paragraph 4 adds a new paragraph 5, which reads as follows: "A precautionary measure for the withdrawal of a driving licence may be imposed on the police for a period of time and in a manner prescribed by a special law. After the expiry of this period, the police may request the Criminal Court to extend the precautionary measure to withdraw the driver's licence if a request is made for the initiation of the infringement procedure." The current paragraph 5 shall become paragraph 6. Explanatory - It was obvious that the applicant's intention was that the police could change the way and duration of the measure of withdrawal of the driver's licence and not all other precautions to result from the first sentence of the proposed amendment in Article 138(4) of the proposal. Thank you very much.

The cause of these proposed changes as well as the new Road Safety Act lies in a large number of road accidents with severe consequences on roads in the Republic of Croatia. The drivers from these countries are not significantly different from the drivers in Croatia in their heads, but rather they are simply trained to behave differently with strict sanctions and strict application of them. As an example which, truth be told, is not a driver's license, but shows how mentality is changing, and how a culture of certain behavior is created, I will say that in England 10 years ago, an anachronous law was abolished by the English Parliament, which prescribed that anyone caught in pushing when entering a tram with a horse's grip be flogged with 12 punches. The Law was from the second half of the 19th century and was in effect for over 100 years. I do not wish to call on these calls that we may have missed such a similar regulation in Croatia, but I would like to point out that sharp sanctions can achieve a preventive purpose and we at the HSS Club are confident that this will contribute with time to change the behaviour of drivers on our roads. Although, as I said, we at the HSS Club welcome the tightening of sanctions provided for by these amendments and additions to the Law, we believe that we should immediately approach the creation of a new, whole Law on Violations, which would equally impose sanctions for violations of all kinds, not just traffic. And today, the minister said that accession is being prepared for a new law, which we welcome. As for increasing the withdrawal of the driver's license from 3 days to 30 days, we join those who believe this is questionable from the point of view of greater depression towards the perpetrators of the most serious traffic offences and that it is more focused on police work. It is also questionable that the time of withdrawal of the driver's licence due to the duration of the infringement procedure may be extended to a much longer period, which is in breach of the Constitution protected citizens' rights. Nevertheless, the HSS Club will support the proposed amendments and additions to the Law on Violations with the proposal to take account of the new full law on violations of the doubt. ParlaMint-HR_2004-07-14-0.u17807	Thank you. Dear Madam Vice President, Mr. Secretary, ladies and gentlemen! Here, let me look back on behalf of the Libre Club on this bill. The Club LIBRE therefore supports the fundamental intention of this law to reduce the number of killed and injured on our roads that this year, as we have heard, has escalated and reached dramatic proportions. The LIBRE Club also supports the law’s intention to focus on offenders who harshly violate traffic regulations, as stated in the reasoning of the criminal policy blade. And here we welcome the intention to strengthen the penalties of those who commit traffic violations because of excessive speed, too much alcohol in the blood, especially those who repeat traffic violations. According to the above data that we have in the proposal for this law, Croatia is one of the most endangered European states for road safety. In the past ten years, there have been more deaths and injuries in traffic accidents than in the Homeland War, and more than 600 deaths are expected in the current year alone. In the first six months, more than 300 people were killed on our roads. This describes enough the weight of the problem that could cause even more trouble with the expected increase in traffic density. The road safety law stipulates that all persons directly and indirectly involved in traffic safety are responsible for the safety of traffic. Unfortunately, this is not the case in practice, so I would like to draw attention to the fact that, for example, according to the research of the Institute of Transport and the links to liability for accidents in Croatia, unconstitutionally and incorrectly is distributed. This actually limits the achievements of fairer measures and the most severe consequences are suffered by drivers and pedestrians. The systematic European research has defined three important causes of the impact of road accidents. These are the first imperfect and misbehavior of road users, drivers and pedestrians, the second tensile imperfection and failure of the vehicle, and the third technical imperfection, the failure of the transport infrastructure. Studies further showed that all three influential factors are about the same as those affecting accidents. The main problem, therefore, is that our traffic operations are aimed at drivers, unconstitutionally and unambiguously, the main responsibility for traffic accidents. Such thinking is most often the result of a desire for problems to be at least declared as simple and as fast as possible. In our opinion, this is unfair not only to traffic users but also to traffic police who are often overly burdened and misdirected. The impact of other factors in the first order of regularity of transport infrastructure and vehicles is generally underestimated and often neglected. For example, it is ignored that inadequate security is often due to insufficient quality, incomplete and unadjusted transport projects. For example, we also have fresh from our tunnels on new highways that pop up, their lids pop out so to speak for themselves from the bed and cause traffic accidents. Also, the increased number of accidents on wet roads is largely due to an unquality asphalt layer and poor drainage of roads. Drivers are often the wrong result of insufficient and unfair direct information from drivers about the dangers ahead, which warns of the lack of standards and unstandard road signalling. Danger often generates an unquality regulation of traffic in traffic networks and at intersections. Also, the safety of transport reduces the poor and unfair maintenance of the stake and traffic signalling. In addition, technical maintenance of vehicles services and technical checks are not significantly lower than in Western European countries but the general state of the rolling stock is nevertheless significantly lower. It is known that the average age of our vehicles is more than ten years ago and also a few days ago we also brought excise duties to collect new cars and thus made it difficult for people to replace old cars with new cars, etc. So these are some facts and reasons for causing insecurity in traffic that we don't think are sufficiently trained in this law. Let me draw particular attention now to one of the most important segments of transport regulation which is well defined and processed in Western European countries and is only open with this law. only some of the articles of this law, for example, Article 221. I'm talking about differentiated traffic regulations that selectively limit the permitted speed. For example, about the night and early morning hours for late and foggy times or the degree of speed-limiting of driving for young drivers with aggregate sanctioning, collecting penalties for general good speed overruns. So here I am talking about Article 211. about an article that treats the problems of young drivers that we would especially like to warn about. Just to point out that we went to Club LIBRE with our Transport Protection Act that we were just about to increase, increase the safety of young drivers. Why young drivers? Because in our opinion, they are the most endangered part of the population that is currently moving on our roads. So there's the data, for example, the most drastic data comes from 2000. There are approximately 250 thousand young drivers in Croatia, which is about 14%, and for example in 2000 similar to in other years, they are relatively much more involved in traffic accidents. So, for example, that year in the total number of car accidents, so the rape of young drivers was about 35%. They were with 35% of the participants in accidents with the deceased, they were participants with 36% in accidents with killed young drivers, and in alcohol-affected accidents they participated with 28.4%. There's got to be similar data this year. We, therefore, have now turned this law into an amendment to Article 221, because the government proposes similar solutions, so we primarily try to prevent this problem from being solved. We believe that our solution that we offer with the amendment is simpler and fairer, so here we have a request for the government to try to consider our solution so that we can really help young drivers in the right way. What exactly is this about? This amendment, therefore, to Article 221 or the new article 221, has five items. In paragraph 1, what is most important is that a driving licence is issued for a period of two years. It is also proposed by the Government but we propose it in this article of the law. We also suggest that persons referred to in paragraph 1 of this law should not manage or start driving a vehicle if there are intoxicating drugs, alcohol or if it shows signs of alcoholisation, so we have the same inside. Furthermore, the persons referred to in paragraph 1 of this Article shall be obliged to mark the vehicle, this is a new, special label, young drivers spoken. So I think it's important because it's easier to control exactly the younger drivers that at random not all the vehicles stop but that if a cop sees a vehicle driving a young driver to stop it and control it. It also refers to a speed limit which is the same as Article 211 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union. Government's proposal. In paragraph 4, there is a speed limit, almost the same. In paragraphs 4 and 5, a period of trials is put in place, which seems to me to be not in your bill of law. Therefore, if, within those two years, the infringements referred to in Articles 2 and 3 are committed or a certain number of points are collected, the trial shall be extended by another year. In Article 5, then, if the driver is young, the driver does not comply with these obligations will need to undergo a repeat driving test. Since the application of certain measures prescribed by the Law of 96, such as the introduction of penalty points, more severe penalties for the harsh violation of traffic rules with the possibility of withdrawing a driving licence at the scene of the infringement, changing the knowledge of traffic rules, and others have seen a significant improvement in road safety. Despite the increase in the number of traffic accidents, there was a reduction in the number of people killed and seriously injured. However, there was a deterioration in road safety in 2003 as seen in the increase in the number of people killed and injured. The following problems are constantly present during the observed period. The increase in the number of road users affected by alcohol accidents, the proportion of road users killed in alcohol accidents, the inappropriate rate of loss of young drivers, the increase in the number of drivers without a driving test, the participation in road traffic of drivers with unregistered or unscathed motor vehicles. I would like to point out that this law we have ahead of us is not aimed at drivers and citizens who respect the law, but has been done on the contrary to increase their safety in transport and to reduce the loss of innocent people. In the Proposal for a Law on Road Safety against the provisions of the current law, the following paper is most important, I will list some, it is special attention to the young drivers from 18 to 20 years old, who now suffer largely in traffic accidents. For young drivers, speed is limited, driving a car of more than 75 kilowatts is prohibited, and driving bans from 23 to 5 hours without escorts. The material provisions of the current text of the law have been corrected and supplemented, so that they are more understandable and clear with a view to greater efficiency for transport safety. Oversight of the work of car schools and driving exams is transferred to the MUP. In the future, driving tests will be carried out at the test centres designated by the MIP where the driver’s licence will be issued. A ban shall be imposed on the use of mobile phones for drivers at the time of driving, and a complete ban shall be imposed on the proportion of alcohol in the body when operated by the vehicle. And the penalty is measured from only fines to maximum penalties provided for by law. By increasing the participation of freight vehicles in traffic accidents, the number of vehicles involved in traffic accidents has been increased, thus increasing the number of those vehicles in the first five months of 2004 by 46.9 compared to the same period in 2003. It can be concluded from all of these indicators that traffic in Slavonia has seen an increase in participation in the transport of freight and connected vehicles resulting in an increase in the number of traffic accidents and victims. The contribution is also to increase the destruction of roads in Slavonia, which must be renewed at the expense of taxpayers. The Podravska highway passes through the settlements and through the narrow centers of municipalities and cities. A large number of cargo vehicles create more and more traffic jams and certainly reduces traffic safety. Mr. Minister, not only you but also Minister Calmett and the Government ask you on behalf of the people of Slavonia and Baranja what you intend to do to remove from the Podravska highway freight vehicles from Serbia, Bulgaria, Romania, Bosnia and Herzegovina. This would certainly contribute to greater traffic security in Slavonia, with this law being passed. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2004-07-14-0.u17927	Thank you, Mr. Vice President of the Parliament, Mr. Minister, members of the government, colleagues and colleagues. The new law free to say is adopted at the end of the day and contains measures of preventive and criminal policy which should be introduced in a hasty manner to change the behaviour of irresponsible road users and, in particular, to change driver behaviour. Responsibility is not an exclusive feature of a driver, but literally of all road users, a car accident can be blamed by anyone. But drivers manage motor vehicles and can do much more damage to other participants. It is necessary to act on everything and especially on them. The law contains large and constructive procedures that directly contribute to greater road safety according to world experience and research, such as the introduction of mandatory use of daily or karat lamps on vehicles, regardless of the conditions of visibility, the ban on the use of mobiles and other devices during driving and other devices. I particularly welcome the introduction of measures relating to the education of new young drivers who do not have the necessary driving experience for safe driving, such as reducing their age from 17.5 to 16 years and 9 months for people who want to start training more of the role of parents for their underage children who are being trained and so on. In particular, it should be commended to the Government of the Republic of Croatia to define and introduce measures aimed at reducing the loss of new drivers on our roads. For example, a speed limit, a ban on organised transport of children, a ban on towing vehicles, a ban on driving at night from 23 to 05 hours except for escorts, a ban on driving a personal car whose engine power exceeds 75 kilowatts and motorcycles whose engine power exceeds 25 kilowatts. I particularly welcome the introduction of a safe driving programme whose implementation should receive a more thorough and overall quality driver education over a period of two years after obtaining a driver's license with a significantly smaller number of young people killed on our roads. In accordance with the practice of the European Union countries, I particularly welcome the opening of the possibility of tightening the criminal policy for serious offences such as driving under the influence of alcohol and for offenders who repeat serious violations by imposing a prison sentence. Unfortunately, without a resolutional criminal policy in our country, it is not possible to change the behaviour of road users, this has been confirmed in practice. Protection of the rights of persons with disabilities to free movement and respect for their specific needs has found places in this regulation, pointing to the desirable social sensitivity of our Government for persons with special needs. Because of these arguments, I strongly support the adoption of this Law in the form in which it is proposed because I am convinced that its application will result in a significant improvement in the safety of traffic on our roads and a reduction in the number of victims. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2004-07-14-0.u18545	Mr. President, ladies and gentlemen of the Parliament. The Act on Scientific Activity and Higher Education, I remind, was passed on July 31st and entered into force on August 15th last year. It has been shown that the Law contains certain shortcomings and discrepancies in a professional and nomotechnical aspect that prevents it from being applied. The fundamental lack of law consists in its practical non-implementability under further circumstances limited to the real state of science and high educational activity and in the law of those deadlines within which the law should be applied. The obvious non-implementability of the law is evident among other things in the fact that some of the deadlines laid down by law were exceeded before the new Government of the Republic of Croatia was able to apply this Law. The fact that the Law was inconclusive also faced the previous Government, which adopted the Regulation amending the Law on Scientific Activity and Higher Education at its last session on 18 December 2003. I recall the Regulation applying the basic provisions on the financing of science and higher education was moved from 1 January to 1 July of this year. The difficulties in the implementation of certain concrete legal solutions, with the lack of compliance of the law with other laws, are insufficient clarity and the need for precision, and nomotechnical irregularities and the need for corrections were manifested in around 40 provisions of this law. They're listed in the introductory text and I think it's easy to identify them. In addition to these individual provisions of the law, the truth does not necessarily require changes and additions, but should certainly be taken into account in some reasonable correction of the law. This group certainly includes poor systematization of certain issues in the law and, in particular, poor language solutions in it. This proposal sought to remove key obstacles to the achievement of the law's task set, and to enable the functioning of the science and higher education systems, to the minimum extent necessary for the adoption of the law. This law, mainly in terms of necessary precision, and the following principle of law enforcement, needs to be regulated, in particular, 15 key issues that I will only briefly comment on. National Council for Science and Higher Education, Agency for Science and Higher Education. The question of evaluation of scientific institutes, higher schools and study programmes. The process of elections in scientific titles, co-operational titles and jobs. A permit to carry out high school activities, education for the needs of the military and the police. The university management system. The names of the bodies of the Faculty of Arts Academys. Professional names and academic degrees. The election process in high schools. A question of the position of assistants, rights issues and obligations of teachers. Budget and finance. Penalty of provisions and safeguards as in the new law. and the question may eventually, but not least, perhaps most importantly, the question of deadlines in transitional and final provisions. So that they enable the unequivocal achievement of the iron and designated reform objectives, with the greatest guarantee of the quality of science and higher education. The adoption of this law will mean creating a series of favourable normative and mainly time frames for the realistic implementation of the transformation of the Croatian System of Scientific and High Educational Activities in terms of the proposed and desirable pro-European integration processes. The transformation processes of these activities will take place in parallel with their relatively regular time and with the minimum unwanted consequences that are normally followed by such root changes indicated by the law and laid down by the law passed a year ago. To note that the implementation of this law does not require the provision of specific additional resources in the state budget of the Republic of Croatia. Why the emergency procedure proposal? Generally, Croatia is late to include these activities in European integration processes. It is therefore necessary to intervene urgently in the legal provisions by regulating a number of deadlines that have already been exceeded. Here, so much for the introduction, thank you very much for your attention.

ParlaMint-HR_2004-07-14-0.u18732	Your Honor, Mr. President, the honourable ladies and gentlemen of the council. We are ahead of us with presidential elections, so the government wants to settle with these two draft laws all of those open issues concerning both the financing of the electoral campaign and the rights of the President of the Republic at the end of office, so they also concern those who will be presidential candidates. The Law on the Special Rights of the President of the Republic, upon termination of our duties, proposes to further strengthen the integrity of the functions of the President of the Republic by creating material and technical assumptions that the President of the Republic may continue with the activities that are appropriate to the former President of the Republic. We suggest that the government provide the former president with physical protection and personal driver and personal official vehicle. We also want it to be prescribed that the President of the Republic is made available at the end of his duty to the office room suitable for the conduct of his activities that he will have at the end of his duties. Two civil servants who will assist the president in his work will also be provided with a burden on the state budget. We suggest that salaries for officials and energy costs, equipment, furniture fall on the burden of the state budget. This right of the former president of the Republic could benefit while he considers it necessary in the government's view it may be permanent but according to the president's opinion it will last as long as he wishes. And we also want to solve a possible open question, and this in the event of the death of the President of the Republic of Croatia, that funeral costs fall on the burden of the Republic of Croatia. The final draft law on the financing of electoral promotion wanted to introduce order in the way of obtaining funds for election promotion. We wanted to define what these funds could be spent on, as well as to attribute certain sanctions if the promotion funds were supplied contrary to the provisions of this Act. Our basic principle that the candidate for President of the Republic can finance his promotion from his own resources and from donations of domestic and foreign natural and legal persons. Of course, after the election results have been announced, the presidential candidate who carries out the legally prescribed majority of votes, which is according to the current 10% vote law, will receive a fee from the state budget for part of the cost of the election pre-demand. And according to the decision on the amount of electoral promotion to be settled from the state budget that the government has made 30 days before the election day. We want to remove the possibility and abuse of positions by this law and also remove the possibility that the elected president will come into conflict with the dependence on the people who made the donation, so we suggest that donations from foreign states and foreign political parties be banned. Of public companies, legal persons with public authority and companies in the majority ownership of the auction. These other legal persons in which the Republic of Croatia or the units of local and regional self-government or regional self-government have majority ownership over shareholders and shares. These public and other institutions owned by the Republic of Croatia and units of local or regional self-government. further, we want to prohibit receiving donations from Synicates and employers' associations. We also want to prohibit receiving donations from trust and foundation associations, whose members are members of state authorities. Or our intention was to eliminate the possibility for the heads of state to manipulate the funds of the associations and finance the campaign against this bill We prohibit and propose that the financing of presidential promotions from the budget of the units of local self-government and the budget of the regional co-government units be prohibited. From the assets of companies owned and co-owned by units of local and regional self-government. This institution which is the founder of states or units of local or regional self-government. The means of electoral promotion could be used to organise promotional events, distribution of promotional materials, production of radio and television videos, and other things commonly considered an election promotion. It is possible that the candidate for President of the Republic will be the current state official. Which benefits from the regular implementation of its activities to fund the state budget, and therefore we want to exclude it by this law, unless in the case of a person protected by a special regulation, he or she has a obligation or a right to physical protection. And he could use what special regulation allows him to use in his promotional travel activities, by coming to various gatherings. Because this regulation is not only a right but also an obligation to protect a national official of a given category. The government has therefore gone from full confidence in the state election commission to overrun the procurement control mechanisms, in such a way as to give great authority to the State Election Commission that it can publish donations that are not in line with this Act. However, in discussions on the Committees, particularly on the Committee on the Constitution of the Rules of Procedure and the political system, this provision has proved to be controversial, therefore it has been sent here to the Government of the Amendment Committee and I trust you and the Government in principle to accept the weight of the part in the content of that amendment. Therefore, I would not like the debate to be burdened with such a proposed solution. Already as one whole text in line with the amendments of the Committee on the Constitution, Rules of Procedure and the political system. In order to address these open issues in time before the official launch of the election promotion, we propose that these laws be adopted by emergency procedure. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2004-07-14-0.u18734	Mr. President, dear colleagues and colleagues. The SDP representative club has been motivated for several reasons to prepare the amendment proposal and the addition of the Law on the election of the President of the Republic. Of course, one that can be pragmatic is that it is nearing the end of the current term to the current elected President of the Republic of Croatia, Mr Stjepan Mesic, and that soon it is expected, and certainly somewhere at the end or early next year, to call the election for the President of the Republic of Croatia again. We believe that issues about elections, rights, obligations and responsibilities should be regulated both by candidates, both parties and all other participants in the election in time so that there should be no and should not be any appropriate irregularities, questions and problems in the framework of the election campaign, or rather, we feel that it is necessary to ensure that the electoral system is familiar, stable and in this respect to ensure fair, fair and legal election promotion, campaign, or overall elections that must enable all candidates to have an equal position, i.e. equality with the law. The second reason that it motivated us is because of this we have just proposed, I will start with this, believing that if the majority party, in principle, has more hands in Parliament, determined not to accept our bill that it will initiate and motivate the majority party, or the government to prepare the necessary amendment and addition of the law. Why? For this reason, another reason that motivated us to approach the amendment of this law, which is, above all, necessary to bring the Law on the election of the President of the Republic of Croatia into line with the new draft Constitution, i.e. the amended Constitution of the Republic of Croatia and, of course, with the decisions of the Constitutional Court, notably with the decision of the Constitutional Court of 11 May 2004 and, of course, the decision of the 2002 Constitutional Court. You see the third reason is the need to align this law with the legal system in relation to laws within the legal system that are sometimes reported as jus generalis or as jus specialis, i.e. as lex specialis. It is therefore necessary to see and for these reasons to bear in mind and to approach the actual arrangement of this amendment and addition. Of course, the fourth reason is materially legal. We felt and felt that it was really necessary to introduce in our electoral system another law, a law on the financing of election campaigns that would regulate the issue of election campaigns of all representatives, i.e. all participants in the elections from local representatives to local, representative bodies of local and regional self-government units until elections for the Croatian Parliament and for the President of the Republic. But until this single law was passed, we felt that it was necessary for the sake of transparency, for the public's insight into the financing of the election campaign only to implement the election campaign, to spend the funds and relations between the candidates, to practically regulate this issue both with the President of the Republic, and in this way we suggested that amendments and additions should be extended, practically complemented by this law and with these issues, and that, of course, as we can see it has already been initiated in some way by the Government to propose its own special law, not by a special chapter, or by a special couple of articles, to settle this issue, so let's go in order. This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. The Constitution of the Republic of Croatia is prescribed that, within 60 days, elections must be held in cases where cases set out in the Constitution referred to in Article 96(3) occur and that is where, because of the permanent prevention or the God of the death of the President of the Republic, the second president shall be replaced, then, within 60 days, the conditions for holding elections must be maintained or must be established. We in the current Law on Elections for the President of the Republic have, in fact, the situation that this was similarly arranged, but this was referred to in Article 97(1). The Constitution, therefore, is quite different from what is now in the Constitution after the amendments to the Constitution and therefore the issue should be settled. The second issue related to compliance with the decisions of the Constitutional Court is the fact that the Constitutional Court has found, among other things, that Articles 44(1) and (2) and 47(1) are unconstitutional and that it is practically necessary to open the space for the President to be able to participate and submit objections to the protection of his rights in relation to the State Election Commission, which has not been explicitly stated under the law, and of course, when it comes to Article 44(1), it is necessary to provide practically all political parties and all other parties who control and these 100 voters with the ability to exercise practical control and to submit objections to the election of the President of the Republic. The third reason for compliance with the laws adopted in the meantime is the fact that we have passed the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Code of Origin and under that law has been abolished for the purposes of reconciling that matter with the constitutional matter on the protection of personal data which the Constitution explicitly prescribes and prescribes when and in what way personal data can be used, and that is explicitly when it prescribes the law on the one hand and allows, on the other hand, when it has the consent of one whose data is collected and, of course, in cases where it is approved by the Ministry of Interior, it is for the purposes of special protection of the special interest of the Republic of Croatia. Furthermore, we believe that in this part we should have resolved the issues that are related and as a legal void, which is, among other things, the issue in fact in the collection of gifts, i.e. donations and other means for implementing not only electoral promotion but practically election campaign presidential candidates, and therefore we propose in our proposal the law first to elect, that giving gifts to the candidate should be prohibited by the authorities of state authorities, bodies of units of local and regional self-government and other legal persons with public authority. It is then necessary to prohibit this from public companies, i.e. companies and other legal entities in which the Republic of Croatia or the units of local and regional regional self-government have majority ownership over shares, i.e. shares and public and other institutions owned by the Republic of Croatia, units of local and regional self-government. Furthermore, it could not be done by associations of citizens and trusts whose members are members of the state authorities of the Republic of Croatia, units of local and regional regional self-government and other legal entities with public authority. Furthermore, we do not believe that gifts should be allowed to be given to candidates by domestic entrepreneurs and legal entities operating with the authorities of the Republic of Croatia in order to obtain profits, i.e. in the last two calendar years, which have received support from the state, local self-government units and areas of regional self-government or other legal persons with public authority who received money from funds in which the state or units of local and regional self-government transferred money from their budget and those who lost their business in the previous year. We also believe that this should be prohibited by the authorities of the state authorities of foreign states and public undertakings, i.e. companies and other legal entities in which foreign states, or units of their local and regional self-government, have majority ownership over shares, i.e. shares and public undertakings owned by foreign governments or owned by units of local self-government as well as foreign nationals themselves. We also think it is very important that the voters of the Republic of Croatia have the possibility of public control in fact the election campaign that takes place and that from the point of view of the collection, so from the point of view of spending money. And finally, fair relations in the election campaign itself. We therefore propose that the candidate for President, the Republic, is required within 8 days of the elections being held, to submit a report to the Electoral Commission on the funds spent on the selection of the promotion. Then the report must contain information on the selection of funds, and in the case of cash or non-money gifts received, data on the donor, monetary value of the gift, etc. We also consider and prescribe, we propose to prescribe that the State Election Commission will publish this information in the National Journal as a summary report with sources of funds, on the funds spent for the election promotion of the President of the Republic. In addition to these issues that we covered, of course, that we covered a very specific issue, which is participation in the financing of election campaign of presidential candidates from the Croatian budget, i.e. units of local self-government. And we think, first of all, that election campaigns need, to spend resources on candidates in some way to limit the instructive norm. And we propose to limit it to 7 million kuna, some believe it is a little too much, some that it is too little, but in any case all are to limit such spending in the election campaign and given the opportunities in which the Republic of Croatia, its citizens, etc. And on the third hand, the fact is it's not small money for an election campaign. Therefore, we believe that it should be limited to 7 million, but we also believe that it is necessary in a certain way to ensure proper sanctions if the candidates do not comply with this restriction. In such a situation, we believe that those candidates who do not adhere to restrictions could not participate in the division of funds allocated from the state budget to assist candidates in the election campaign because of their own wasteful behaviour in the election campaign itself. We also believe that there is another way to regulate participation in the division of funds. So far, it is legal to see that every candidate who has crossed at least 10% of the electoral threshold, so he would have had to have the opportunity to participate in those funds and receive compensation for those funds and they were generally shared with those candidates equally. We believe it is not correct to give the same than to apply the electoral criterion. Because each candidate, depending on how much he won the fallen votes, should actually enable him to participate in the funding of the state budget that is being financed and thus, practically stimulated by the one who received the most, which is ultimately given to citizens and voters the highest degree of trust. We support the firm idea that appeared in our political and democratic practice from different institutions and the institution of justice that it is necessary to adopt in Croatia as soon as possible a law on a permanent electoral commission that would be permanent, professional and that would, in an appropriate way, actually follow and conduct all elections. But we also believe that until a new law on permanent election committees is passed that it cannot be left to guess who is president of the electoral commission in the election for the President of the Republic of Croatia. And that is why we suggest that it be until such a law is passed, the President of the Supreme Court. Furthermore, we propose to align this law with the whole, with the terminology that is necessary and not only for nomotechnical but also for substantive reasons, and it is related to the amendments and additions to the Law on Government, the Law establishing the Ministry, administrative and other organisations. Then it is related to harmonising with the Law on Croatian Television and the Law on Elections that they have accepted, and they have stated that we do not have an electoral commission of the Republic of Croatia, but the State Election Commission, so to speak, etc., etc. And therefore for nomotechnical reasons. From all these situations, we conclude very simply. The fact is that some of the provisions we have proposed in the amendments and additions to this Act are now covered by the proposal for a law on the financing of election promotions by the President of the Republic of Croatia. But it is also a fact that some important provisions that are not large are not covered, and it is necessary to make them in order to comply with the Constitution of the Republic of Croatia, the Constitutional Court's decision. And in this respect, we are fully aware that, given the opinion of the Government of the Republic of Croatia, there is a certain danger that, in fact, after the debate, no decision on acceptance will be taken but the most likely decision on non-acceptation will be taken because the Government does not support this Amendment and Supplements Act on the election of the President of the Republic of Croatia. Seven votes in, two votes against. After the debate, the Committee proposed by a majority of votes, 8 for, 1 vote against, to the Croatian Parliament to pass the proposed law. As regards the Law on the financing of electoral promotion for the election of the President of the Republic of Croatia, the law in the debate supported the motion for the motion to pass the law by an urgent procedure with 7 votes in favour and 2 votes against. In the debate, the Committee raised its comments on the text of the final law and established its amendments, and following a debate with the majority of votes 6 for and 3 abstentions, proposed to the Croatian Parliament to pass the law with the amendments that we have explained in this opinion. The amendments refer to Article 2 where we are actually regulating this issue with an international practice where actually foreign natural and legal persons are not allowed to participate in the financing of election promotion. Amendment number 3 is, in fact, explained and specified in Article 3. A government proposal that actually regulates which gifts are not to be received. Article 4 also clarifies that electoral promotion within the meaning of this law is not only considered a media promotion but also all the costs of candidates from election day to election day. The most important article is Article 6 which we have actually changed de facto the whole article. We propose to amend the Government's proposal where we ask that the issue of transparency and the public be properly addressed to the level and sources of funds used by each candidate for the election promotion. And under this third law, the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on the Election of Presidents of the Republic of Croatia, the Committee concluded not to accept the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements with 6 votes to and 2 votes against but we expressed our opinion that the Government is considering the need for the necessary amendments and additions to the applicable Act to comply with the Constitutional Court's decision and the applicable legislation. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2004-07-14-0.u18744	Thank you, Mr. President. Mr. Secretary of State, colleagues and fellow MPs! Well, here we have a whole set of laws relating to the election of the president of the state, but I would look a little more closely at the proposal for a Law on the financing of election promotion for the election of the President of the Republic of Croatia. If we keep in mind the goal that the law should achieve, then we must say that we have a very strange proposal, which is why it is very superficial, does not regulate key areas and where it is trying to regulate to a large extent is unclear. The aim of the law should be to ensure transparency of funding for the presidential candidate. The aim will not be achieved by this law, and the public's interest will continue to be compromised. The public interest is to know who it is and what amounts it has financed the future president of the state who will represent the state on behalf of the citizens in the next term. Under this law, citizens will still not know this because it is proposed to regulate only the issues of how to raise funds for the financing of electoral promotion and the activities considered to be election promotion rather than election campaigns as a whole. Article 2 of the Law states that election promotion is financed from own resources and donations from domestic and foreign natural and legal persons, however, the law does not define the concept of donations. Donations can be different. They can be in money, things, services, rights, etc. For full transparency, a clear definition of the donation as well as the determination of the maximum amount of the donation is necessary. In our opinion, the Law also needs to determine the time considered an election campaign. The law stipulates which donations are not to be received, so it says no donations from foreign states and foreign political parties are to be received with which we fully agree. Neither public undertakings, legal persons with public authority and state-owned enterprises are to be accepted, with which we also agree. However, donations shall not be received from associations, foundations and foundations whose members are members of the state authorities of the Republic of Croatia, local and regional self-government bodies and other legal entities with public authority. We wonder how in practice this regulation is enforceable. The assumption of enforceability is the possibility of control and control is not possible because there is no single register of membership of the implementation associations of foundations and foundations. By accepting this provision, we would get another regulation that would be a dead letter on paper. I remind you that we already have laws that prohibit certain appointments of persons who are members of political parties and yet are regularly appointed party members, and only because it is the only relevant evidence of party membership of the candidate himself that he is not a member of any party. The Pharisee's law doesn't need it, it doesn't belong there, and that's why we throw it out not only of this but also of all the laws. So it seems indisputable that this law must ensure transparency of funding. The Proposer obviously believes that this transparency is provided by Article 6 which states that election reports for election costs must be submitted to the State Electoral Commission no later than 7 days before the election day, which will therefore assess the need to publish them. Then, the state electoral commission will make public donations contrary to the law no later than three days before the day of the elections. This article does not guarantee transparency for at least two reasons. First, the reports contain only sources of funding but not the names and last names of individual donors and the amount and type of donation. Secondly, the report shall contain data no later than 7 days before the date of the election. What about the sources and amounts that appear in the last seven days? These donations may be more than previously received and they do not even have to apply. What about the donations after the election? They don't have to apply either. Third, there is no obligation to publish these missing data, but the State Election Commission will assess the need for publication. Is that transparency? Is it the realisation of the right of citizens to know who he is and how he financed the election promotion of representative candidates? In particular, paragraph 2 is unacceptable, which says that the state electoral commission will make public donations contrary to the law no later than three days before the elections. It is a source of uncertainty and is also very suitable for manipulations and abuses that can have a crucial impact on election results. The state electoral commission is not infallible and its decision is not irrefutable. This is also evidenced by the practice and would be too dangerous to allow the State Election Commission to announce three days before the election the content of its decision on the counter-legislative nature of the donation which, in the follow-up procedure, may prove unfounded, incorrect, inexplicable, etc. For the candidate who lost the election repairs because of the announcement, there is no one. By not being allowed and simply amazing that such a proposal was found in a law addressed to the emergency procedure. Speaking of urgency, I must say that there is no single reason for the adoption of neither this Act nor the Law on the Rights of the President nor this proposal on the election of the President of the Republic of Croatia, the third proposal that we have received. In this regard, the Rules of Procedure of the Parliament in Article 159 states: "Extremely, the law can only be passed by emergency procedure when the interests of defence and other particularly justified state reasons, i.e. where necessary to prevent or eliminate major disturbances in the economy.". So there is no reason, and this is the ridiculous explanation of the proposings, and this is the Government of the Republic of Croatia when it says: "The need for a precise definition of election promotion for the election of the President of the Republic of Croatia has been assessed as sufficient reason for the urgency of the procedure." It is regrettable, though I am a member of the Committee that the Committee on the Constitution, Rules of Procedure and the political system, and the Parliament itself has accepted his imposed opinion of the Government and the Parliament itself is acting against the Rules of Procedure at this time. As it is apparent the remarks are many and they are well-founded. It would be wise to discuss this issue in detail and pass a law that we won't be ashamed of. We have experience with the Law of Conflict of Interest that was brought into the atmosphere of pre-election passion, not reason. Today, it is increasingly said that it is a conceptual failure, and therefore let us not slave with passions, let us put the ball on the ground, let us think carefully to find the best solutions and then pass the Law in a regular procedure. Such a bill as the proposed Club of Representatives of the Croatian Peasant Party cannot support. Although he believes the Law on the financing of the election campaign is absolutely necessary. As regards the remaining two draft laws, regarding the right of the President of the Republic of Croatia, as I have explained, we oppose the adoption of an emergency procedure because it is indeed a violation of the Rules of Procedure if we do not keep the integrity of this Assembly alone, there is no one in the state of Croatia who will protect our digntet and therefore we stick to what we ourselves have prescribed. We suggest that the government provide the former president with physical protection and personal driver and personal official vehicle. ParlaMint-HR_2004-07-14-0.u18770	Mr. President, dear colleagues and colleagues. This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. The Constitution of the Republic of Croatia is prescribed that, within 60 days, elections must be held in cases where cases set out in the Constitution referred to in Article 96(3) occur and that is where, because of the permanent prevention or the God of the death of the President of the Republic, the second president shall be replaced, then, within 60 days, the conditions for holding elections must be maintained or must be established. We in the current Law on Elections for the President of the Republic have, in fact, the situation that this was similarly arranged, but this was referred to in Article 97(1). The Constitution, therefore, is quite different from what is now in the Constitution after the amendments to the Constitution and therefore the issue should be settled. The second issue related to compliance with the decisions of the Constitutional Court is the fact that the Constitutional Court has found, among other things, that Articles 44(1) and (2) and 47(1) are unconstitutional and that it is practically necessary to open the space for the President to be able to participate and submit objections to the protection of his rights in relation to the State Election Commission, which has not been explicitly stated under the law, and of course, when it comes to Article 44(1), it is necessary to provide practically all political parties and all other parties who control and these 100 voters with the ability to exercise practical control and to submit objections to the election of the President of the Republic. The third reason for compliance with the laws adopted in the meantime is the fact that we have passed the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Code of Origin and under that law has been abolished for the purposes of reconciling that matter with the constitutional matter on the protection of personal data which the Constitution explicitly prescribes and prescribes when and in what way personal data can be used, and that is explicitly when it prescribes the law on the one hand and allows, on the other hand, when it has the consent of one whose data is collected and, of course, in cases where it is approved by the Ministry of Interior, it is for the purposes of special protection of the special interest of the Republic of Croatia. Furthermore, we believe that in this part we should have resolved the issues that are related and as a legal void, which is, among other things, the issue in fact in the collection of gifts, i.e. donations and other means for implementing not only electoral promotion but practically election campaign presidential candidates, and therefore we propose in our proposal the law first to elect, that giving gifts to the candidate should be prohibited by the authorities of state authorities, bodies of units of local and regional self-government and other legal persons with public authority. It is then necessary to prohibit this from public companies, i.e. companies and other legal entities in which the Republic of Croatia or the units of local and regional regional self-government have majority ownership over shares, i.e. shares and public and other institutions owned by the Republic of Croatia, units of local and regional self-government. Furthermore, it could not be done by associations of citizens and trusts whose members are members of the state authorities of the Republic of Croatia, units of local and regional regional self-government and other legal entities with public authority. Furthermore, we do not believe that gifts should be allowed to be given to candidates by domestic entrepreneurs and legal entities operating with the authorities of the Republic of Croatia in order to obtain profits, i.e. in the last two calendar years, which have received support from the state, local self-government units and areas of regional self-government or other legal persons with public authority who received money from funds in which the state or units of local and regional self-government transferred money from their budget and those who lost their business in the previous year. We also believe that this should be prohibited by the authorities of the state authorities of foreign states and public undertakings, i.e. companies and other legal entities in which foreign states, or units of their local and regional self-government, have majority ownership over shares, i.e. shares and public undertakings owned by foreign governments or owned by units of local self-government as well as foreign nationals themselves. We also think it is very important that the voters of the Republic of Croatia have the possibility of public control in fact the election campaign that takes place and that from the point of view of the collection, so from the point of view of spending money. And finally, fair relations in the election campaign itself. We therefore propose that the candidate for President, the Republic, is required within 8 days of the elections being held, to submit a report to the Electoral Commission on the funds spent on the selection of the promotion. In addition to these issues that we covered, of course, that we covered a very specific issue, which is participation in the financing of election campaign of presidential candidates from the Croatian budget, i.e. units of local self-government. And we think, first of all, that election campaigns need, to spend resources on candidates in some way to limit the instructive norm. And we propose to limit it to 7 million kuna, some believe it is a little too much, some that it is too little, but in any case all are to limit such spending in the election campaign and given the opportunities in which the Republic of Croatia, its citizens, etc. And on the third hand, the fact is it's not small money for an election campaign. Therefore, we believe that it should be limited to 7 million, but we also believe that it is necessary in a certain way to ensure proper sanctions if the candidates do not comply with this restriction. In such a situation, we believe that those candidates who do not adhere to restrictions could not participate in the division of funds allocated from the state budget to assist candidates in the election campaign because of their own wasteful behaviour in the election campaign itself. We also believe that there is another way to regulate participation in the division of funds. So far, it is legal to see that every candidate who has crossed at least 10% of the electoral threshold, so he would have had to have the opportunity to participate in those funds and receive compensation for those funds and they were generally shared with those candidates equally. We believe it is not correct to give the same than to apply the electoral criterion. Because each candidate, depending on how much he won the fallen votes, should actually enable him to participate in the funding of the state budget that is being financed and thus, practically stimulated by the one who received the most, which is ultimately given to citizens and voters the highest degree of trust. We support the firm idea that appeared in our political and democratic practice from different institutions and the institution of justice that it is necessary to adopt in Croatia as soon as possible a law on a permanent electoral commission that would be permanent, professional and that would, in an appropriate way, actually follow and conduct all elections. But we also believe that until a new law on permanent election committees is passed that it cannot be left to guess who is president of the electoral commission in the election for the President of the Republic of Croatia. And that is why we suggest that it be until such a law is passed, the President of the Supreme Court. Furthermore, we propose to align this law with the whole, with the terminology that is necessary and not only for nomotechnical but also for substantive reasons, and it is related to the amendments and additions to the Law on Government, the Law establishing the Ministry, administrative and other organisations. Then it is related to harmonising with the Law on Croatian Television and the Law on Elections that they have accepted, and they have stated that we do not have an electoral commission of the Republic of Croatia, but the State Election Commission, so to speak, etc., etc. And therefore for nomotechnical reasons. From all these situations, we conclude very simply. The fact is that some of the provisions we have proposed in the amendments and additions to this Act are now covered by the proposal for a law on the financing of election promotions by the President of the Republic of Croatia. But it is also a fact that some important provisions that are not large are not covered, and it is necessary to make them in order to comply with the Constitution of the Republic of Croatia, the Constitutional Court's decision. And in this respect, we are fully aware that, given the opinion of the Government of the Republic of Croatia, there is a certain danger that, in fact, after the debate, no decision on acceptance will be taken but the most likely decision on non-acceptation will be taken because the Government does not support this Amendment and Supplements Act on the election of the President of the Republic of Croatia. But at the same time, we feel that it is necessary to be fully aware of the real situation in which we are for formal and content reasons. Because it is necessary to make these amendments in order to ensure fair and fair law, and based on the law and the Constitution, a legally executed election for the President of the Republic of Croatia.
