ParlaMint-HR_2004-07-14-0.u18779	Thank you very much. On behalf of the HSS Club, respected representative Ljubica Lalic. Here you go.
ParlaMint-HR_2004-07-14-0.u18780	Thank you, Mr. President. Mr. Secretary of State, colleagues and fellow MPs! Well, here we have a whole set of laws relating to the election of the president of the state, but I would look a little more closely at the proposal for a Law on the financing of election promotion for the election of the President of the Republic of Croatia. If we keep in mind the goal that the law should achieve, then we must say that we have a very strange proposal, which is why it is very superficial, does not regulate key areas and where it is trying to regulate to a large extent is unclear. The aim of the law should be to ensure transparency of funding for the presidential candidate. The aim will not be achieved by this law, and the public's interest will continue to be compromised. The public interest is to know who it is and what amounts it has financed the future president of the state who will represent the state on behalf of the citizens in the next term. Under this law, citizens will still not know this because it is proposed to regulate only the issues of how to raise funds for the financing of electoral promotion and the activities considered to be election promotion rather than election campaigns as a whole. Article 2 of the Law states that election promotion is financed from own resources and donations from domestic and foreign natural and legal persons, however, the law does not define the concept of donations. Donations can be different. They can be in money, things, services, rights, etc. For full transparency, a clear definition of the donation as well as the determination of the maximum amount of the donation is necessary. In our opinion, the Law also needs to determine the time considered an election campaign. The law stipulates which donations are not to be received, so it says no donations from foreign states and foreign political parties are to be received with which we fully agree. Neither public undertakings, legal persons with public authority and state-owned enterprises are to be accepted, with which we also agree. However, donations shall not be received from associations, foundations and foundations whose members are members of the state authorities of the Republic of Croatia, local and regional self-government bodies and other legal entities with public authority. We wonder how in practice this regulation is enforceable. The assumption of enforceability is the possibility of control and control is not possible because there is no single register of membership of the implementation associations of foundations and foundations. By accepting this provision, we would get another regulation that would be a dead letter on paper. I remind you that we already have laws that prohibit certain appointments of persons who are members of political parties and yet are regularly appointed party members, and only because it is the only relevant evidence of party membership of the candidate himself that he is not a member of any party. The Pharisee's law doesn't need it, it doesn't belong there, and that's why we throw it out not only of this but also of all the laws. So it seems indisputable that this law must ensure transparency of funding. The Proposer obviously believes that this transparency is provided by Article 6 which states that election reports for election costs must be submitted to the State Electoral Commission no later than 7 days before the election day, which will therefore assess the need to publish them. Then, the state electoral commission will make public donations contrary to the law no later than three days before the day of the elections. This article does not guarantee transparency for at least two reasons. First, the reports contain only sources of funding but not the names and last names of individual donors and the amount and type of donation. Secondly, the report shall contain data no later than 7 days before the date of the election. What about the sources and amounts that appear in the last seven days? These donations may be more than previously received and they do not even have to apply. What about the donations after the election? They don't have to apply either. Third, there is no obligation to publish these missing data, but the State Election Commission will assess the need for publication. Is that transparency? Is it the realisation of the right of citizens to know who he is and how he financed the election promotion of representative candidates? In particular, paragraph 2 is unacceptable, which says that the state electoral commission will make public donations contrary to the law no later than three days before the elections. It is a source of uncertainty and is also very suitable for manipulations and abuses that can have a crucial impact on election results. The state electoral commission is not infallible and its decision is not irrefutable. This is also evidenced by the practice and would be too dangerous to allow the State Election Commission to announce three days before the election the content of its decision on the counter-legislative nature of the donation which, in the follow-up procedure, may prove unfounded, incorrect, inexplicable, etc. For the candidate who lost the election repairs because of the announcement, there is no one. By not being allowed and simply amazing that such a proposal was found in a law addressed to the emergency procedure. Speaking of urgency, I must say that there is no single reason for the adoption of neither this Act nor the Law on the Rights of the President nor this proposal on the election of the President of the Republic of Croatia, the third proposal that we have received. In this regard, the Rules of Procedure of the Parliament in Article 159 states: "Extremely, the law can only be passed by emergency procedure when the interests of defence and other particularly justified state reasons, i.e. where necessary to prevent or eliminate major disturbances in the economy.". So there is no reason, and this is the ridiculous explanation of the proposings, and this is the Government of the Republic of Croatia when it says: "The need for a precise definition of election promotion for the election of the President of the Republic of Croatia has been assessed as sufficient reason for the urgency of the procedure." It is regrettable, though I am a member of the Committee that the Committee on the Constitution, Rules of Procedure and the political system, and the Parliament itself has accepted his imposed opinion of the Government and the Parliament itself is acting against the Rules of Procedure at this time. As it is apparent the remarks are many and they are well-founded. It would be wise to discuss this issue in detail and pass a law that we won't be ashamed of. We have experience with the Law of Conflict of Interest that was brought into the atmosphere of pre-election passion, not reason. Today, it is increasingly said that it is a conceptual failure, and therefore let us not slave with passions, let us put the ball on the ground, let us think carefully to find the best solutions and then pass the Law in a regular procedure. Such a bill as the proposed Club of Representatives of the Croatian Peasant Party cannot support. Although he believes the Law on the financing of the election campaign is absolutely necessary. As regards the remaining two draft laws, regarding the right of the President of the Republic of Croatia, as I have explained, we oppose the adoption of an emergency procedure because it is indeed a violation of the Rules of Procedure if we do not keep the integrity of this Assembly alone, there is no one in the state of Croatia who will protect our digntet and therefore we stick to what we ourselves have prescribed.
